`INTEGRATION.md`:

```md
# Integrating the overlay into the launcher

What the launcher side needs to know to ship and start the overlay agent. For the agent's internals,
see [README.md](README.md). Paths like `packages/core/...` are in the launcher repository
(`hhhhhffd/_k0t1k`, checked out as `_k0t1k-launcher`).

## TL;DR

1. Get the jar onto the user's disk under a **versioned file name**. The section on distribution
   below explains why the version must be in the name.
2. When launching an instance with the overlay enabled, add two JVM arguments through
   `LaunchOptions::java_agent_args` (`packages/core/src/launch/mod.rs:49`):

   ```text
   -javaagent:<absolute path to k0t1k-overlay-<version>.jar>=lang=<en|ru>
   -Dk0t1k.overlay.dataDir=<absolute path to the overlay data folder>
   ```

3. Nothing else is needed. There is no IPC yet: the overlay reads the game's own `logs/latest.log`
   and keeps its state in `dataDir`.

## The artifact

- **Build:** run `./gradlew shadowJar`. It needs JDK 21 or newer to build; the Gradle wrapper and
  toolchain handle the rest. It does not need a shader compiler, because the SPIR-V is committed.
- **Output:** `build/libs/k0t1k-overlay-<version>.jar`, about 5.4 MB. The
  version comes from `build.gradle.kts` (`version = "0.2.1"`) and also appears in the manifest as
  `Implementation-Version`.
- **Contents:** one jar for every OS, CPU, loader and Minecraft version. There are no natives
  inside; it uses the LWJGL the game ships. Byte Buddy is shaded under
  `dev.k0t1k.overlay.shadow.bytebuddy`.
- **Runtime:** Java 8 to 25.

## Launch arguments

`java_agent_args` already carries authlib-injector for ely.by accounts. Append the overlay's two
arguments to the same list; the order between the agents does not matter. `build_command` puts the
list before the profile's JVM arguments, which is what a `-javaagent` needs.

| Argument | Notes |
|---|---|
| `-javaagent:<jar>=lang=ru` | Use the absolute jar path. Put only simple `key=value,key=value` options after the `=`; the agent splits that text on commas. |
| `-Dk0t1k.overlay.dataDir=<dir>` | Pass paths as system properties. A system property wins over the agent argument and can contain commas and spaces. `std::process::Command` passes each argument verbatim, so no quoting is needed. |
| `-Dk0t1k.overlay.lang=ru` | Equivalent to `lang=` in the agent argument. |
| `-Dk0t1k.overlay.gameDir=<dir>` | Only needed if the working directory is not the game folder. The launcher already starts the game in `game_dir`, so leave it out. |

**Language:** the launcher forces `-Duser.language=en`, so the agent cannot detect the language by
itself. Pass the launcher's UI language: `ru` or `en`. Anything else falls back to English.

**Username:** the agent reads `--username` from the game arguments to detect mentions in chat. That
already works, so nothing has to be passed.

## The data folder

Recommendation: one shared folder in the launcher's data directory, for example
`<launcher data>/overlay/`. That fits the "everything next to the exe" rule and gives one chat
history and one set of notes across all instances, which is the point of the feature. The default,
when `dataDir` is not passed, is `<gameDir>/k0t1k-overlay`, a separate folder per instance.

The agent creates these files on demand:

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| `chat-history.tsv` | Every chat line ever received, one per line: `epoch ms TAB server TAB text`. Backslash escapes tab, newline and itself. Append-only, so it grows forever, by roughly 100 bytes per message. |
| `notes/<id>.txt` | One UTF-8 file per note. |
| `overlay.properties` | HUD on/off and its corner. |

- Several games can run at the same time with the same `dataDir`. Each appends to the history in
  one write per batch.
- Two games started from the **same game folder** both read the same `latest.log` and store every
  line twice. That only matters for "run a second copy of this instance" if it reuses the folder.
- **Privacy:** the history keeps private messages and server chat in plain text with no limit. The
  launcher should say so where the overlay is switched on, and should offer a way to clear it:
  deleting `chat-history.tsv` while no game is running is enough.

## Distribution and updates

The JVM keeps the agent jar open for the whole session, and **on Windows the file is locked**.
Therefore:

- Never overwrite the jar in place. Store it as `k0t1k-overlay-<version>.jar` and point new
  launches at the new file.
- Delete old versions only when no game is running. The launcher already detects running games
  through java processes.

The jar ships the same way as the RatScan and packconv plugins:

- `scripts/release-plugin.sh` publishes a GitHub Release of this repository with `plugin.json`,
  `plugin.json.sig` and `k0t1k-overlay.jar`. The manifest carries the jar's SHA-256 and is signed with
  the plugin key (`_k0t1k-plugin\n` + the exact bytes of `plugin.json`), so one signature covers the
  release. The version is `version` in `build.gradle.kts`; bump it before releasing.
- The launcher build fetches the latest signed release (`apps/app/scripts/fetch-plugins.mjs`) into
  `apps/app/src-tauri/plugins/overlay/` and embeds that jar in the binary as the baseline, so release
  builds need no JDK and the overlay works offline.
- At run time the launcher's update check installs newer signed releases into
  `<launcher data>/plugins/overlay/<version>/`. Each launch copies the newest valid copy into the
  overlay data folder as `k0t1k-overlay-<version>.jar` and points the JVM at it.

## What to expect at runtime

- **Hotkey:** Shift+Tab opens and closes the overlay, Esc closes it. The hotkey is not
  configurable yet. While a game text field is focused, Shift+Tab still goes to the overlay.
- **Supported setups:**
  - Minecraft 1.8–1.12.2 on LWJGL 2 with OpenGL
  - Minecraft 1.13–26.1 on GLFW with OpenGL
  - 26.2 on GLFW with OpenGL or Vulkan
  - 26.3+ on SDL3 with OpenGL or Vulkan
  - VulkanMod
  - vanilla, Fabric, Forge and NeoForge

  The README has the exact test matrix.
- **Unsupported setups are harmless.** On Minecraft before 1.8 the hooks may not match and the agent
  does nothing. The same happens with a renderer the agent cannot draw into, such as a queue without
  graphics support, an OpenGL below 3.0 on LWJGL 2, or macOS before 1.17 (LWJGL 3 compatibility
  context). So the launcher can add the agent unconditionally when the user enables the feature.
  `k0t1k_core::overlay::supports` gates it to 1.8 and newer, and hiding the option for older versions
  is cleaner still.
- **Separate clients:** Lunar and Feather are started by their own launchers, so the overlay does
  not apply to them. LabyMod 4 in the client slot is untested.
- **Cost:** while the overlay is closed and the HUD is off, the game's frame is untouched; the hooks
  only time frames and read the log every 250 ms. Memory is about 10 MB of direct buffers, plus a
  few MB of GPU memory under Vulkan.
- **Startup:** Byte Buddy installs its hooks before the game's `main`, which adds a fraction of a
  second.

## Log lines

Everything the agent prints is prefixed `[k0t1k-overlay]` and goes to the game's stdout/stderr, so
it shows up in the launcher's game log:

| Line | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `[k0t1k-overlay] started, data in <dir>` | The agent is running. It appears when the first hooked LWJGL call happens. |
| `[k0t1k-overlay] disabled, could not start: ...` | The agent could not load. The game runs as if the agent were not there. |
| `[k0t1k-overlay] disabled after an error:` plus a stack trace | The overlay switched itself off mid-game; the game keeps running. Worth a bug report. |
| `[k0t1k-overlay] <exception>` | A recoverable error, for example a failed disk write. The overlay keeps running. |

The launcher could watch for `disabled` and show a small notice.

**Java 24 and newer** print a JDK warning, `sun.misc.Unsafe::objectFieldOffset has been called by
dev.k0t1k.overlay.shadow.bytebuddy...`. It is harmless and comes from Byte Buddy. On Minecraft 26.x
the JDK prints the same kind of warning for LWJGL's own `System::load`.

## Testing an integration

1. Launch an instance with the overlay and look for `[k0t1k-overlay] started` in the game log.
2. In game, press Shift+Tab: the overlay should open. Join a server or open a world and check that
   chat messages appear in the Chat tab and in `<dataDir>/chat-history.tsv`.
3. Restart the game: the history and the notes should still be there.

Run these tests only on a virtual display (Xvfb) in WSL, never on the user's desktop, as the rest
of the project does. The agent was tested this way. Two environment problems can get in the way:

- **LWJGL 2 (1.8 to 1.12.2) on Xvfb** lists display modes by running the `xrandr` command, so it
  needs one on `PATH` (a script that prints one mode is enough), and the game window needs X input
  focus (`xdotool windowfocus`), or the game never grabs the mouse. Java 8 is needed: 1.8 to 1.16
  run on it.
- **Mesa 26.2.5** makes Minecraft hang in its own texture upload, with or without the agent. The
  machine was rolled back to 26.2.2.
- **26.3 with OpenGL** cannot create its window on Xvfb, because Xvfb offers no sRGB-capable GLX
  visual. Use `--graphicsBackend vulkan` there.

## Not there yet

- **No channel to the launcher.** A local WebSocket was discussed as the transport for launcher
  features inside the game, such as chat with friends, support tickets or the resource pack
  converter. It is not designed or implemented. The user will specify it.
- **No settings from the launcher** beyond `lang` and `dataDir`. The HUD on/off setting lives in
  `overlay.properties`. Neither the hotkey nor the enable switch can be set in the agent; the
  launcher decides whether to add the agent at all.

```

`README.md`:

```md

  </p>
  <p>
    <a href="https://github.com/hhhhhffd/_k0t1k-overlay/releases/latest">Download</a> ·

## Summary

An overlay for Minecraft Java in the spirit of the Steam overlay. It is a Java agent, not a mod, so one
jar works on vanilla, Fabric, Forge and NeoForge, with OpenGL or Vulkan (Minecraft's own Vulkan backend
or VulkanMod), on LWJGL 2 (Minecraft 1.8 to 1.12.2), GLFW or SDL3 (Minecraft 26.3+).

The agent hooks LWJGL with Byte Buddy and never touches Minecraft classes. It draws over the finished
frame and takes input before the game sees it. It ships with the
[_k0t1k launcher](https://github.com/hhhhhffd/_k0t1k) as a signed plugin, but any launcher can add it with `-javaagent`.

Press **Shift+Tab** in game to open it, and **Shift+Tab** or **Esc** to close it.

## Features

| Tab / feature | What it does |
|---|---|
| **Chat** | The whole chat history across sessions, servers and instances, with full-text search and filters: mentions, private messages, one player's messages. Click a message to copy it. Messages that mention the player's name show a toast. |
| **Notes** | Notes that persist on disk and save automatically. |
| **Tools** | Overworld ↔ Nether coordinates (with "my position"), stacks and shulker boxes, timers and alarms that survive a restart, the latest screenshots. Each F2 screenshot shows a toast with the instance and position. |
| **Mods** | The instance's mods with a search and what each one does (from the mod's metadata), with links to Modrinth and the mod's homepage. |
| **Profile** | Lifetime stats (play time, sessions, deaths, advancements, screenshots, crashes) and the launcher's achievements on top of them. |
| **Performance** | FPS, frame time, 1% low, a frame time graph, heap, GC and the game's CPU load, plus an optional HUD in a corner of the screen. |
| **Browser** | A real Chromium (JCEF, off-screen rendering) in its own tab: back, forward, reload, an address bar that also searches, quick links (Minecraft Wiki, Modrinth, CurseForge, YouTube). |
| **Replay** | The last 30 seconds kept in memory; **Shift+F2** or the button in Tools saves them as a GIF or a video. |
| **Music** | What the system plays (Spotify, a browser tab, any player) with previous, play/pause and next, and a toast when a new track starts. |
| **Crash window** | After a crash, shows the reason and the report and uploads them to mclo.gs only when the player clicks "Send to support". |
| **Cat** | The launcher's cat sits in a corner and reacts to deaths, advancements, a five minute AFK, timers, screenshots and achievements with a speech bubble (can be turned off in Profile). |

The player's position, dimension and health are read by reflection on `net.minecraft` classes, so they
only work where the game runs with Mojang names: Minecraft 26.1+ and NeoForge. Elsewhere the position
button is hidden and deaths are not counted. Advancements are read from the chat.

<details>
<summary>Feature details: browser, replay, music, crash window</summary>

- **Browser.** Mouse, wheel and keyboard go to the page; logins and cookies persist in
  `<dataDir>/browser/profile`. Chromium is about 170 MB, so it is not in the agent: the first time the
  player clicks "Download the browser", `Browser` fetches `me.friwi:jcef-api` and the platform's
  `jcef-natives-*` (jcefmaven's builds) from Maven Central, checks each against the SHA-256 pinned in
  the code and unpacks them into `<dataDir>/browser`. The bridge (`src/cef`: `OverlayBrowser`, a
  `CefBrowser_N` that keeps its pixels instead of painting into AWT, and `CefEngine`) ships inside the
  agent as `cef-bridge.jar` and loads next to JCEF in a class loader of its own. Windows and Linux on
  x64; the page is drawn by the OpenGL renderer only. CEF needs `--no-first-run` off screen: its EULA
  dialog would otherwise end the browser process with exit code 28.
- **Replay.** Frames are kept as JPEG (about 1 MB a second) and saved to `<gameDir>/replays` as a GIF
  or a video (MP4 when `ffmpeg` is on PATH, otherwise a Motion-JPEG AVI). Frames are scaled down on the
  GPU and read back through pixel buffers with fences, so the game never waits for them. OpenGL only.
- **Music.** Windows uses the system media controls through a PowerShell child (`media.ps1`), Linux
  uses `playerctl`.
- **Crash window.** When the game exits after writing a crash report, the shutdown hook writes what
  only the live JVM knows (a dump of every thread, uncaught exceptions, the overlay's notes on the
  session) to `<dataDir>/crashes/` and starts `dev.k0t1k.overlay.CrashDialog` in a JVM of its own. After
  the upload it opens the support Discord with the link copied.

</details>

## Technical Stack

| Group | Technology | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| Core | Java, compiled with `--release 8` | Runs on Java 8 to 25; built with a JDK 21 toolchain |
| Core | Byte Buddy 1.18.16 | Shaded and relocated to `dev.k0t1k.overlay.shadow.bytebuddy`, so it cannot clash with mods |
| Graphics | LWJGL 3.1.6 (main), 3.3.1 (Vulkan), 3.4.3 (SDL), 2.9.3 (LWJGL 2) | `compileOnly`: the game supplies LWJGL |
| Graphics | GLSL → SPIR-V | `src/main/shaders`, compiled with `glslc` and committed |
| Text | stb_truetype (LWJGL 3), java.awt (LWJGL 2) | Font: Inter, SIL OFL 1.1 |
| Browser | JCEF (jcefmaven) | Downloaded at run time, not bundled |
| Build | Gradle 9.7.1, Shadow 9.6.1 | One fat jar for every OS, CPU, loader and version; no natives inside |

## Architecture

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph JVM["Game JVM"]
        Agent["Agent.premain<br/>(-javaagent)"] -->|installs hooks| BB["Byte Buddy advice<br/>on LWJGL classes"]
        Game["Minecraft"] -->|swap / present / input calls| BB
        BB -->|"{class, method, args}"| Disp["Dispatcher"]
        Disp -->|child-first loader<br/>parent = LWJGL's loader| Overlay["Overlay<br/>(dev.k0t1k.overlay.impl)"]
        Overlay --> Batch["Batch<br/>(CPU: SDF rects, glyph atlas)"]
        Batch --> GL["GlRenderer /<br/>LegacyGlRenderer"]
        Batch --> VK["VkRenderer"]
    end
    Log["logs/latest.log"] -->|every 250 ms| Overlay
    Overlay <-->|history, notes, stats| Data[("dataDir")]
    Launcher["Launcher"] -->|"-javaagent:k0t1k-overlay-&lt;version&gt;.jar"| Agent
```

Every hooked method calls one dispatcher with `{class, method, arguments}`. Before the call, the
dispatcher can replace the arguments or skip the call. After the call, it can replace the return value.

| Hook | Purpose |
|---|---|
| `glfwSwapBuffers`, `SDL_GL_SwapWindow` | OpenGL: draws the overlay over the finished frame |
| `vkQueuePresentKHR` | Vulkan: draws into the swapchain image right before it is presented |
| `vkCreateSwapchainKHR`, `vkDestroySwapchainKHR`, `vkDestroyDevice`, `vkGetDeviceQueue(2)` | Vulkan: tracks swapchains and queue families, and adds colour attachment usage to swapchains (Minecraft only blits into them) |
| `glfwSet{Key,Char,CharMods,MouseButton,CursorPos,Scroll}Callback` | GLFW: wraps the game's callbacks, so the overlay sees input first and swallows it while open |
| `SDL_PollEvent` | SDL: the overlay inspects each event and neutralises the ones it takes by rewriting their type to `SDL_EVENT_USER`, which the game ignores |
| `glfwGetKey`, `glfwGetMouseButton` | GLFW: keys and buttons read as released while the overlay is open |
| `Display.update(boolean)` | LWJGL 2: draws before the buffers are swapped (`update()` only calls it, and `processMessages` runs after the swap) |
| `Keyboard.next`, `Mouse.next` | LWJGL 2: after the game reads an event, the overlay looks at it and, if it takes it, reads the next one in its place |
| `Keyboard.isKeyDown`, `Mouse.isButtonDown`, `Mouse.getDX/getDY/getDWheel` | LWJGL 2: keys and buttons read as released and the mouse as still while the overlay is open |
| `Mouse.setGrabbed` | LWJGL 2: holds back the game's grab changes until the overlay closes |
| `glfwSetInputMode(GLFW_CURSOR)`, `SDL_SetWindowRelativeMouseMode`, `SDL_WarpMouseInWindow`, `SDL_Start/StopTextInput` | Holds back the game's cursor and text input changes until the overlay closes |

A key or button release still reaches the game if the game saw the press, so held keys do not stick.

Only calls made on the render thread are filtered. Ixeris polls GLFW or SDL on a thread of its own and
repeats the render thread's window calls there, the overlay's own cursor calls included; those repeats
pass untouched, or the overlay would hold back its own cursor release (tested with Ixeris 4.6.10 on
Fabric 26.2).

### Rendering

Each frame is built once on the CPU as a `Batch`: rounded rectangles as signed distances, glyphs
rasterised on demand, and the glyph atlas. On LWJGL 3 the glyphs come from stb_truetype, which every
Minecraft since 1.13 ships; LWJGL 2 has none, so there `AwtFont` rasterises them with java.awt. A
renderer for the game's graphics API then draws the batch:

- **`GlRenderer`** (OpenGL 3.2 core) saves and restores all GL state it touches, because Minecraft
  caches GL state.
- **`LegacyGlRenderer`** is the same renderer written against LWJGL 2's GL classes. It needs OpenGL 3.0
  (GLSL 1.30, or 1.50 from OpenGL 3.2) in the game's compatibility context.
- **`VkRenderer`** records one render pass over the swapchain image (load, draw, back to
  `PRESENT_SRC_KHR`). It submits on the presenting queue, waits on the semaphores the game passed to the
  present, and makes the present wait on its own semaphore instead.

### Chat history

The chat history comes from `logs/latest.log`: the game writes every chat line there as `[CHAT] ...`,
and `Connecting to host, port` gives the server. Minecraft 1.8.9 and 1.12.2 write the same lines
(`[12:00:00] [Client thread/INFO]: [CHAT] ...`). New lines go to an append-only store, which is read
back a page at a time.

### Design decisions

- **A Java agent hooking LWJGL, not a mod.** Minecraft classes are obfuscated differently per version
  and loader; LWJGL's API is stable. One jar therefore covers vanilla, Fabric, Forge and NeoForge from
  1.8 to 26.3+.
- **Advice sees only JDK types; the overlay loads child-first under LWJGL's loader.** The advice uses
  only JDK types stored in the system properties, which every class loader can see. The overlay links
  against the game's own LWJGL, including inside Forge/NeoForge module layers.
- **Compile against the oldest LWJGL, backends in their own source sets.** The main code compiles
  against LWJGL 3.1.6 (Minecraft 1.13): from 3.2 on javac binds inherited GL methods to `GL30`, which
  3.1 lacks. Every LWJGL member it references was checked to resolve in 3.1.6, 3.2.2, 3.3.3 and 3.4.0.
  Vulkan, SDL and LWJGL 2 live in `src/vulkan`, `src/sdl`, `src/lwjgl2`, each against the LWJGL it
  needs, and are loaded by name.

## Supported versions

| Setup | Window | Graphics | Tested in game |
|---|---|---|---|
| Minecraft 1.8 to 1.12.2 | LWJGL 2 | OpenGL (compatibility context) | vanilla 1.8.9 and 1.12.2 |
| Minecraft 1.13 to 26.1 | GLFW | OpenGL | vanilla 1.21.4, NeoForge 21.4 (1.21.4) |
| Minecraft 26.2 | GLFW | OpenGL or Vulkan | Vulkan |
| Minecraft 26.3+ | SDL3 | OpenGL or Vulkan | Vulkan |
| VulkanMod (Fabric) | GLFW | Vulkan | 0.6.8 on 1.21.11 |

<details>
<summary>Test notes and limits per backend</summary>

- The Vulkan runs were on lavapipe (Mesa's software Vulkan) under Xvfb. They were checked with the
  Khronos validation layer, synchronization validation included, while opening the overlay, typing
  (atlas uploads) and resizing the window (swapchain recreation). The layer reported nothing that came
  from the overlay. VulkanMod's own errors were reported both with and without it. If a game presents
  on a queue family without graphics support, the overlay logs that once and draws nothing.
- On SDL, OpenGL is not tested in game: Xvfb has no sRGB-capable GLX visual, so 26.3 cannot create its
  OpenGL window there. The parts are tested separately: SDL input on 26.3 with Vulkan, and the GL
  renderer on GLFW.
- LWJGL 2 games (1.8 to 1.12.2) were tested on Linux with Mesa llvmpipe (OpenGL 4.6 compatibility
  context) under Xvfb, on Java 8: opening and closing with Shift+Tab and Esc, mouse cursor release and
  re-grab (checked with `XGrabPointer`), no camera jump after closing, a key held through the overlay
  does not stick, typing with key repeat, Cyrillic text, the clipboard, and the chat history from
  `latest.log`. Not tested: Windows, Forge 1.12.2 (its loading screen draws on a second GL context, so
  the renderer is recreated when `GLContext.getCapabilities()` changes; that path never ran), other 1.8
  to 1.12 releases, and any OpenGL below 3.0, where the overlay logs an error and disables itself.
  Versions before 1.8 are not supported. Two things differ from LWJGL 3: the HiDPI scale is always 1,
  and glyphs are not kerned. The glyph rasteriser uses java.awt (no window is opened).
- Before 1.17 the game runs in a compatibility context. macOS only offers OpenGL 2.1 there, so the
  overlay's GLSL 1.50 shaders do not compile and the overlay disables itself.
- Runs on Java 8 to 25 (compiled with `--release 8`). Minecraft 1.8 to 1.16 uses Java 8.

</details>

## Project Structure

```text
.
├── build.gradle.kts         # source sets, shading, manifest (Premain-Class), version
├── src/
│   ├── main/java            # Agent, dispatcher, overlay UI, GL renderer, GLFW platform
│   ├── main/resources       # Inter font, committed SPIR-V, cat sprite, media.ps1
│   ├── main/shaders         # GLSL sources for the Vulkan renderer
│   ├── vulkan/java          # VkRenderer (LWJGL 3.3.1)
│   ├── sdl/java             # SdlPlatform (LWJGL 3.4.3, Minecraft 26.3+)
│   ├── lwjgl2/java          # LegacyPlatform, LegacyGlRenderer (Minecraft 1.8 to 1.12.2)
│   └── cef/java             # JCEF bridge, shipped inside the agent as cef-bridge.jar
├── scripts/
│   └── release-plugin.sh    # signed GitHub release for the _k0t1k launcher
├── INTEGRATION.md           # what a launcher needs to ship and start the agent
└── docs/mascot.png          # the cat
```

## Build

```sh
./gradlew shadowJar   # build/libs/k0t1k-overlay-<version>.jar
```

It needs JDK 21 or newer; the Gradle wrapper and toolchain handle the rest. No shader compiler is
needed because the SPIR-V is committed. After editing `src/main/shaders`, run `./gradlew compileShaders`
(it needs `glslc` from shaderc on PATH).

### Release

`scripts/release-plugin.sh` publishes the jar as a signed launcher plugin: a GitHub Release with
`plugin.json`, `plugin.json.sig` and `k0t1k-overlay.jar`. The release version is `version` in
`build.gradle.kts`; bump it first. The _k0t1k launcher embeds the latest release as a baseline and
installs newer ones through its update check (see [INTEGRATION.md](INTEGRATION.md)).

> [!WARNING]
> The script needs a clean, pushed tree, `gh` signed in, and the plugin signing key
> (`$K0T1K_PLUGIN_KEY`, default `~/.config/k0t1k/plugin-signing-key.pem`). The key never goes into a
> repository; the script checks the signature against the public key the launcher embeds.

## Launching

Add the agent to the JVM arguments:

```text
-javaagent:/path/to/k0t1k-overlay.jar=lang=ru
```

Options are `key=value` pairs separated by commas after `=`. Each can also be set with a system property
`-Dk0t1k.overlay.<key>=...`, which wins and suits paths that contain commas.

| Option | Default | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `dataDir` | `<gameDir>/k0t1k-overlay` | Where the chat history, notes and settings live. Point every instance at one folder to share them |
| `gameDir` | working directory | The game folder holding `logs/latest.log` |
| `lang` | `en` | `en` or `ru` |
| `crashDialog` | `true` | `false` turns off the overlay's crash window, for a launcher that shows its own |

> [!NOTE]
> On Windows the JVM locks the agent jar for the whole session. A launcher should store each version
> under its own file name and never overwrite a jar in place.

<details>
<summary>The data folder</summary>

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| `chat-history.tsv` | One message per line, `epoch ms TAB server TAB text`, where backslash escapes tab, newline and itself |
| `notes/<id>.txt` | One note per file |
| `overlay.properties` | The HUD and cat settings |
| `stats.properties` | Lifetime counters and unlocked achievements; each game adds its own changes, so games running at once do not overwrite each other |
| `session-<hash>.lock` | Present while a game folder is running; one left behind counts as a crash |
| `timers.tsv` | `due epoch ms TAB length ms TAB label` |
| `screenshots.tsv` | A caption per screenshot, `file TAB epoch ms TAB instance TAB dimension TAB x y z` |
| `crashes/crash_<time>.txt` | The crash window's notes from the live game |
| `browser/` | The downloaded JCEF (`jcef-api.jar`, `cef-bridge.jar`, `cef-152.0.6/`) and the browser profile |

Besides these, the agent writes only saved replays to `<gameDir>/replays`. On exit, a shutdown hook
stores the last chat lines, any unsaved notes and the stats.

</details>

If anything fails, the agent prints `[k0t1k-overlay] ...` with a stack trace to the game's output and
disables itself, and the game keeps running.

> [!WARNING]
> The chat history keeps private messages and server chat in plain text with no limit. Deleting
> `chat-history.tsv` while no game is running clears it.

## Known limitations

- Shift+Tab always goes to the overlay, even while a game text field has focus, so the game's reverse
  tab completion does not get it.
- Two games started from the same game folder both read its `latest.log`, so they store every message
  twice.
- Any message containing the player's name counts as a mention, including death messages.

## License

[GNU Affero General Public License v3.0](LICENSE). The bundled Inter font is under the SIL Open Font
License 1.1 (`src/main/resources/dev/k0t1k/overlay/impl/Inter-LICENSE.txt`).

```

`build.gradle.kts`:

```kts
plugins {
    java
    id("com.gradleup.shadow") version "9.6.1"
}

group = "dev.k0t1k"
version = "0.2.1"

repositories {
    mavenCentral()
}

// The game supplies LWJGL, so the overlay only compiles against the oldest one it runs on (Minecraft
// 1.13). That matters beyond API: from 3.2 on GL30 extends GL11, so compiling against a newer LWJGL
// would bind GL11 methods as GL30.glEnable etc., which 3.1 does not have.
val lwjgl = "3.1.6"

// Backends that only exist in newer games compile against the oldest LWJGL that has them, in their
// own source sets, and are loaded by name, so the main code never links against them.
// Vulkan: Minecraft 1.20+ (VulkanMod) and 26.2+ ship LWJGL 3.3.1 or newer.
val lwjglVulkan = "3.3.1"
// SDL: Minecraft 26.3+.
val lwjglSdl = "3.4.3"
// LWJGL 2 (Minecraft 1.12.2 and older, Java 8): another API, so another source set.
val lwjgl2 = "2.9.3"
// The in-game browser's JCEF (jcefmaven's build on Maven Central). The overlay downloads the same
// version at run time (Browser.VERSION, with its SHA-256 pinned there); keep the two in step.
val jcef = "jcef-17e805a+cef-152.0.6+g708dc14+chromium-152.0.7977.83"

sourceSets {
    create("vulkan") {
        compileClasspath += sourceSets.main.get().output
    }
    create("sdl") {
        compileClasspath += sourceSets.main.get().output
    }
    create("lwjgl2") {
        compileClasspath += sourceSets.main.get().output
    }
    // The bridge to JCEF. It must load next to JCEF (its classes live in JCEF's own package), so it
    // ships as a jar inside the agent, not as classes of it.
    create("cef") {
        compileClasspath += sourceSets.main.get().output
    }
}

dependencies {
    implementation("net.bytebuddy:byte-buddy:1.18.16")
    compileOnly("org.lwjgl:lwjgl:$lwjgl")
    compileOnly("org.lwjgl:lwjgl-glfw:$lwjgl")
    compileOnly("org.lwjgl:lwjgl-opengl:$lwjgl")
    compileOnly("org.lwjgl:lwjgl-stb:$lwjgl")
    "vulkanCompileOnly"("org.lwjgl:lwjgl:$lwjglVulkan")
    "vulkanCompileOnly"("org.lwjgl:lwjgl-vulkan:$lwjglVulkan")
    "sdlCompileOnly"("org.lwjgl:lwjgl:$lwjglSdl")
    "sdlCompileOnly"("org.lwjgl:lwjgl-sdl:$lwjglSdl")
    "sdlCompileOnly"("org.lwjgl:lwjgl-opengl:$lwjglSdl")
    "lwjgl2CompileOnly"("org.lwjgl.lwjgl:lwjgl:$lwjgl2")
    "cefCompileOnly"("me.friwi:jcef-api:$jcef") {
        isTransitive = false
    }
}

java {
    toolchain {
        languageVersion = JavaLanguageVersion.of(21)
    }
}

tasks.withType<JavaCompile>().configureEach {
    // Minecraft 1.13-1.16 still runs on Java 8.
    options.release = 8
    options.encoding = "UTF-8"
    options.compilerArgs.addAll(listOf("-Xlint:all", "-Xlint:-options", "-Xlint:-classfile"))
}

tasks.jar {
    enabled = false
}

val cefBridgeJar by tasks.registering(Jar::class) {
    archiveFileName = "cef-bridge.jar"
    destinationDirectory = layout.buildDirectory.dir("cef")
    from(sourceSets["cef"].output)
}

tasks.shadowJar {
    from(sourceSets["vulkan"].output, sourceSets["sdl"].output, sourceSets["lwjgl2"].output)
    from(cefBridgeJar) {
        into("dev/k0t1k/overlay/impl")
    }
    archiveBaseName = "k0t1k-overlay"
    archiveClassifier = ""
    // Mods may bring their own Byte Buddy; ours must never clash with it.
    relocate("net.bytebuddy", "dev.k0t1k.overlay.shadow.bytebuddy")
    // Byte Buddy's Java 24 classes (its JDK class file API bridge) are left out: Forge 1.8.9-1.12.2 scans
    // the agent jar with an old ASM that fails on them. Byte Buddy uses its shaded ASM without them.
    exclude("META-INF/versions/**", "module-info.class", "META-INF/maven/**")
    manifest {
        attributes(
            "Premain-Class" to "dev.k0t1k.overlay.Agent",
            "Implementation-Version" to project.version,
        )
    }
}

// The SPIR-V in src/main/resources is committed, so building needs no shader compiler. After editing
// src/main/shaders, run this task (it needs glslc from shaderc on PATH).
val compileShaders by tasks.registering(Exec::class) {
    val out = "src/main/resources/dev/k0t1k/overlay/impl"
    commandLine("sh", "-c",
        "glslc -O src/main/shaders/overlay.vert -o $out/overlay.vert.spv && " +
            "glslc -O src/main/shaders/overlay.frag -o $out/overlay.frag.spv")
}

tasks.assemble {
    dependsOn(tasks.shadowJar)
}

```

`gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.properties`:

distributionBase=GRADLE_USER_HOME
distributionPath=wrapper/dists
distributionUrl=https\://services.gradle.org/distributions/gradle-9.7.1-bin.zip
networkTimeout=10000
retries=0
retryBackOffMs=500
validateDistributionUrl=true
zipStoreBase=GRADLE_USER_HOME
zipStorePath=wrapper/dists

```

`gradlew`:

```
#!/bin/sh

#
# Copyright © 2015 the original authors.
#
# Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
# you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
# You may obtain a copy of the License at
#
#      https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
#
# Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
# distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
# WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
# See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
# limitations under the License.
#
# SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
#

##############################################################################
#
#   gradlew start up script for POSIX generated by Gradle.
#
#   Important for running:
#
#   (1) You need a POSIX-compliant shell to run this script. If your /bin/sh is
#       noncompliant, but you have some other compliant shell such as ksh or
#       bash, then to run this script, type that shell name before the whole
#       command line, like:
#
#           ksh gradlew
#
#       Busybox and similar reduced shells will NOT work, because this script
#       requires all of these POSIX shell features:
#         * functions;
#         * expansions «$var», «${var}», «${var:-default}», «${var+SET}»,
#           «${var#prefix}», «${var%suffix}», and «$( cmd )»;
#         * compound commands having a testable exit status, especially «case»;
#         * various built-in commands including «command», «set», and «ulimit».
#
#   Important for patching:
#
#   (2) This script targets any POSIX shell, so it avoids extensions provided
#       by Bash, Ksh, etc; in particular arrays are avoided.
#
#       The "traditional" practice of packing multiple parameters into a
#       space-separated string is a well documented source of bugs and security
#       problems, so this is (mostly) avoided, by progressively accumulating
#       options in "$@", and eventually passing that to Java.
#
#       Where the inherited environment variables (DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS,
#       and GRADLE_OPTS) rely on word-splitting, this is performed explicitly;
#       see the in-line comments for details.
#
#       There are tweaks for specific operating systems such as AIX, CygWin,
#       Darwin, MinGW, and NonStop.
#
#   (3) This script is generated from the Groovy template
#       https://github.com/gradle/gradle/blob/<unknown>/platforms/jvm/plugins-application/src/main/resources/org/gradle/api/internal/plugins/unixStartScript.txt
#       within the Gradle project.
#
#       You can find Gradle at https://github.com/gradle/gradle/.
#
##############################################################################

# Attempt to set APP_HOME

# Resolve links: $0 may be a link
app_path=$0

# Need this for daisy-chained symlinks.
while
    APP_HOME=${app_path%"${app_path##*/}"}  # leaves a trailing /; empty if no leading path
    [ -h "$app_path" ]
do
    ls=$( ls -ld "$app_path" )
    link=${ls#*' -> '}
    case $link in             #(
      /*)   app_path=$link ;; #(
      *)    app_path=$APP_HOME$link ;;
    esac
done

# This is normally unused
# shellcheck disable=SC2034
APP_BASE_NAME=${0##*/}
# Discard cd standard output in case $CDPATH is set (https://github.com/gradle/gradle/issues/25036)
APP_HOME=$( cd -P "${APP_HOME:-./}" > /dev/null && printf '%s\n' "$PWD" ) || exit

# Use the maximum available, or set MAX_FD != -1 to use that value.
MAX_FD=maximum

warn () {
    echo "$*"
} >&2

die () {
    echo
    echo "$*"
    echo
    exit 1
} >&2

# OS specific support (must be 'true' or 'false').
cygwin=false
msys=false
darwin=false
nonstop=false
case "$( uname )" in                #(
  CYGWIN* )         cygwin=true  ;; #(
  Darwin* )         darwin=true  ;; #(
  MSYS* | MINGW* )  msys=true    ;; #(
  NONSTOP* )        nonstop=true ;;
esac



# Determine the Java command to use to start the JVM.
if [ -n "$JAVA_HOME" ] ; then
    if [ -x "$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java" ] ; then
        # IBM's JDK on AIX uses strange locations for the executables
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/jre/sh/java
    else
        JAVACMD=$JAVA_HOME/bin/java
    fi
    if [ ! -x "$JAVACMD" ] ; then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: $JAVA_HOME

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
else
    JAVACMD=java
    if ! command -v java >/dev/null 2>&1
    then
        die "ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH.

Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the
location of your Java installation."
    fi
fi

# Increase the maximum file descriptors if we can.
if ! "$cygwin" && ! "$darwin" && ! "$nonstop" ; then
    case $MAX_FD in #(
      max*)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -H is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        MAX_FD=$( ulimit -H -n ) ||
            warn "Could not query maximum file descriptor limit"
    esac
    case $MAX_FD in  #(
      '' | soft) :;; #(
      *)
        # In POSIX sh, ulimit -n is undefined. That's why the result is checked to see if it worked.
        # shellcheck disable=SC2039,SC3045
        ulimit -n "$MAX_FD" ||
            warn "Could not set maximum file descriptor limit to $MAX_FD"
    esac
fi

# Collect all arguments for the java command, stacking in reverse order:
#   * args from the command line
#   * the main class name
#   * -classpath
#   * -D...appname settings
#   * --module-path (only if needed)
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and GRADLE_OPTS environment variables.

# For Cygwin or MSYS, switch paths to Windows format before running java
if "$cygwin" || "$msys" ; then
    APP_HOME=$( cygpath --path --mixed "$APP_HOME" )

    JAVACMD=$( cygpath --unix "$JAVACMD" )

    # Now convert the arguments - kludge to limit ourselves to /bin/sh
    for arg do
        if
            case $arg in                                #(
              -*)   false ;;                            # don't mess with options #(
              /?*)  t=${arg#/} t=/${t%%/*}              # looks like a POSIX filepath
                    [ -e "$t" ] ;;                      #(
              *)    false ;;
            esac
        then
            arg=$( cygpath --path --ignore --mixed "$arg" )
        fi
        # Roll the args list around exactly as many times as the number of
        # args, so each arg winds up back in the position where it started, but
        # possibly modified.
        #
        # NB: a `for` loop captures its iteration list before it begins, so
        # changing the positional parameters here affects neither the number of
        # iterations, nor the values presented in `arg`.
        shift                   # remove old arg
        set -- "$@" "$arg"      # push replacement arg
    done
fi


# Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS='"-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"'

# Collect all arguments for the java command:
#   * DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS, JAVA_OPTS, and optsEnvironmentVar are not allowed to contain shell fragments,
#     and any embedded shellness will be escaped.
#   * For example: A user cannot expect ${Hostname} to be expanded, as it is an environment variable and will be
#     treated as '${Hostname}' itself on the command line.

set -- \
        "-Dorg.gradle.appname=$APP_BASE_NAME" \
        -jar "$APP_HOME/gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar" \
        "$@"

# Stop when "xargs" is not available.
if ! command -v xargs >/dev/null 2>&1
then
    die "xargs is not available"
fi

# Use "xargs" to parse quoted args.
#
# With -n1 it outputs one arg per line, with the quotes and backslashes removed.
#
# In Bash we could simply go:
#
#   readarray ARGS < <( xargs -n1 <<<"$var" ) &&
#   set -- "${ARGS[@]}" "$@"
#
# but POSIX shell has neither arrays nor command substitution, so instead we
# post-process each arg (as a line of input to sed) to backslash-escape any
# character that might be a shell metacharacter, then use eval to reverse
# that process (while maintaining the separation between arguments), and wrap
# the whole thing up as a single "set" statement.
#
# This will of course break if any of these variables contains a newline or
# an unmatched quote.
#

eval "set -- $(
        printf '%s\n' "$DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS $JAVA_OPTS $GRADLE_OPTS" |
        xargs -n1 |
        sed ' s~[^-[:alnum:]+,./:=@_]~\\&~g; ' |
        tr '\n' ' '
    )" '"$@"'

exec "$JAVACMD" "$@"

```

`gradlew.bat`:

```bat
@rem
@rem Copyright 2015 the original author or authors.
@rem
@rem Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
@rem you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
@rem You may obtain a copy of the License at
@rem
@rem      https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
@rem
@rem Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
@rem distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
@rem WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
@rem See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
@rem limitations under the License.
@rem
@rem SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
@rem

@if "%DEBUG%"=="" @echo off
@rem ##########################################################################
@rem
@rem  gradlew startup script for Windows
@rem
@rem ##########################################################################

@rem Set local scope for the variables, and ensure extensions are enabled
setlocal EnableExtensions

set DIRNAME=%~dp0
if "%DIRNAME%"=="" set DIRNAME=.
@rem This is normally unused
set APP_BASE_NAME=%~n0
set APP_HOME=%DIRNAME%

@rem Resolve any "." and ".." in APP_HOME to make it shorter.
for %%i in ("%APP_HOME%") do set APP_HOME=%%~fi

@rem Add default JVM options here. You can also use JAVA_OPTS and GRADLE_OPTS to pass JVM options to this script.
set DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS="-Xmx64m" "-Xms64m"

@rem Find java.exe
if defined JAVA_HOME goto findJavaFromJavaHome

set JAVA_EXE=java.exe
%JAVA_EXE% -version >NUL 2>&1
if %ERRORLEVEL% equ 0 goto execute

echo. 1>&2
echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is not set and no 'java' command could be found in your PATH. 1>&2
echo. 1>&2
echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the 1>&2
echo location of your Java installation. 1>&2

"%COMSPEC%" /c exit 1

:findJavaFromJavaHome
set JAVA_HOME=%JAVA_HOME:"=%
set JAVA_EXE=%JAVA_HOME%/bin/java.exe

if exist "%JAVA_EXE%" goto execute

echo. 1>&2
echo ERROR: JAVA_HOME is set to an invalid directory: %JAVA_HOME% 1>&2
echo. 1>&2
echo Please set the JAVA_HOME variable in your environment to match the 1>&2
echo location of your Java installation. 1>&2

"%COMSPEC%" /c exit 1

:execute
@rem Setup the command line



@rem Execute gradlew
@rem endlocal doesn't take effect until after the line is parsed and variables are expanded
@rem which allows us to clear the local environment before executing the java command
endlocal & "%JAVA_EXE%" %DEFAULT_JVM_OPTS% %JAVA_OPTS% %GRADLE_OPTS% "-Dorg.gradle.appname=%APP_BASE_NAME%" -jar "%APP_HOME%\gradle\wrapper\gradle-wrapper.jar" %* & call :exitWithErrorLevel

:exitWithErrorLevel
@rem Use "%COMSPEC%" /c exit to allow operators to work properly in scripts
"%COMSPEC%" /c exit %ERRORLEVEL%

```

`scripts/release-plugin.sh`:

```sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Publishes the overlay agent as a _k0t1k launcher plugin: a GitHub Release of
# this repo with plugin.json, plugin.json.sig and the agent jar.
# Requires JDK 21, openssl, gh (authenticated) and the plugin signing key.
set -euo pipefail

REPO="hhhhhffd/_k0t1k-overlay"
PLUGIN_ID="overlay"
API_VERSION=1
MIN_LAUNCHER="0.1.4"
JAR_ASSET="k0t1k-overlay.jar"
# Raw 32-byte Ed25519 public key (base64) embedded in the launcher.
PUBLIC_KEY_B64="b37Axim0D7VgDNWVIgvlnpdn2nVOMSLM/jIFbRFc/4k="
KEY="${K0T1K_PLUGIN_KEY:-$HOME/.config/k0t1k/plugin-signing-key.pem}"

die() { echo "release-plugin: $*" >&2; exit 1; }

cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)"

[ -r "$KEY" ] || die "signing key not readable: $KEY"
[ -z "$(git status --porcelain)" ] || die "working tree is not clean"

upstream="$(git rev-parse --abbrev-ref --symbolic-full-name '@{u}' 2>/dev/null)" \
    || die "current branch has no upstream"
git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD "$upstream" \
    || die "HEAD is not pushed to $upstream"
head_sha="$(git rev-parse HEAD)"

version="$(sed -n 's/^version = "\(.*\)"$/\1/p' build.gradle.kts)"
[[ "$version" =~ ^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+$ ]] || die "unexpected version: $version"
tag="v$version"

git rev-parse -q --verify "refs/tags/$tag" >/dev/null && die "local tag $tag already exists"
[ -z "$(git ls-remote --tags origin "refs/tags/$tag")" ] || die "remote tag $tag already exists"
if gh release view "$tag" --repo "$REPO" >/dev/null 2>&1; then
    die "release $tag already exists"
fi

work="$(mktemp -d)"
trap 'rm -rf "$work"' EXIT
out="$work/out"
mkdir "$out"

# No daemon: a lingering Gradle JVM would look like a running game to the launcher.
./gradlew clean shadowJar --no-daemon --quiet
[ -z "$(git status --porcelain)" ] || die "build modified the working tree"
cp "build/libs/k0t1k-overlay-$version.jar" "$out/$JAR_ASSET"

jar_sha="$(sha256sum "$out/$JAR_ASSET" | cut -d' ' -f1)"

# Written once; the signature covers these exact bytes.
cat > "$out/plugin.json" <<JSON
{
  "id": "$PLUGIN_ID",
  "version": "$version",
  "api": $API_VERSION,
  "minLauncher": "$MIN_LAUNCHER",
  "files": {
    "$JAR_ASSET": "$jar_sha"
  }
}
JSON

{ printf '_k0t1k-plugin\n'; cat "$out/plugin.json"; } > "$work/msg"
openssl pkeyutl -sign -rawin -inkey "$KEY" -in "$work/msg" | base64 -w0 > "$out/plugin.json.sig"

# Verify against the launcher's public key: SPKI DER = fixed Ed25519 prefix + raw key.
{ printf '\x30\x2a\x30\x05\x06\x03\x2b\x65\x70\x03\x21\x00'; printf '%s' "$PUBLIC_KEY_B64" | base64 -d; } > "$work/pub.der"
base64 -d "$out/plugin.json.sig" > "$work/sig.bin"
openssl pkeyutl -verify -rawin -pubin -keyform DER -inkey "$work/pub.der" \
    -in "$work/msg" -sigfile "$work/sig.bin" >/dev/null \
    || die "signature does not verify against the launcher public key (wrong signing key?)"

gh release create "$tag" \
    --repo "$REPO" \
    --target "$head_sha" \
    --title "Overlay $tag" \
    --notes "In-game overlay agent $version (plugin API $API_VERSION, built from $head_sha)." \
    "$out/plugin.json" "$out/plugin.json.sig" "$out/$JAR_ASSET"

echo "released $tag"

```
